We can implement "multiplicative Weiszfeld" iteration:
Given p, compute c=p*p. For active/top weights w over lags (sum 1), gradient g_i = ∂/∂p_i ∑ w_k c_k = ∑_k w_k (a_{k-i}+a_{i-k}). Update p_i ← p_i exp( -η g_i ) (or / (1+η g_i/W)) and normalize. This is exponentiated gradient. The factor exp(-η g_i) reduces coordinates that contribute to high lags. Since g_i nonnegative (w≥0, p≥0), all new p smaller, normalized redistributes mass to low g. This may flatten. We can also use target M: choose g_i = max(0, ∑ active where c close to M) etc.

Exact local moves:
- We maintain a normalized a (sum S maybe 1). c exact.
- Objective M=max c.
- Active peaks K = indices where c close to M (within eps). We want reduce them.
- For each coordinate i, its contribution vector to active peaks: D_i(K) = a_{k-i}+a_{i-k}. Moving mass from i to j changes c by δ(D_j-D_i). We can find optimal δ by minimizing max over all k of c + δ(D_j-D_i).
- Candidate j: low D_j at active peaks and not high elsewhere. Source: high D_i at active peaks.

Let's design local optimizer carefully.

We can normalize to sum=1, objective O=2n max_c. Let work with sum=1. For a given a, compute c=np.convolve via FFT or direct? For only final evaluate use direct? In our optimization use FFT. Values positive. We'll maintain c vector length L=2n-1. M=max c, O=2nM.

Coordinate descent move with exact optimal delta using active set.

Key candidate generation:
- We have active indices K where c close to M (e.g., weight w_k = max(0, 1 - (M-c_k)/gap) maybe top R, R=20-100). For source: high D_i(K) where D_i = a_{k-i}+a_{i-k}. Target: low D_i(K).
- Also consider endpoints? Since maxima often near central and tail. Include all indices with c near top 1% sorted.
- For a given i and active weights, score_i = ∑_{r∈K} w_r (a_{r-i}+a_{i-r}). This is a vector computed as weighted cross-correlation of weights with a. We can compute efficiently via FFT: gradient g = ∇ ∑ w_r c_r = 2 convolution? Specifically ∂/∂a_i ∑ w_r c_r = (w*a)_i + (w_rev*a_rev?) Maybe use formula D_i? If we have weights w_r over r=0..L-1, then gradient array G_i = ∑_r w_r (a_{r-i}+a_{i-r}) = (w * a?) Let's derive: ∑_r w_r a_{r-i} with r-i∈[0,n-1] => ∑_{j} a_j w_{j+i} = (rev?); second ∑ w_r a_{i-r}= (w? * a). This can be computed via FFT for one step. But for active K R small, can compute directly O(Rn) if R~50 ->400k, fine. Or use FFT each iteration.

- Candidate sources: top A of score_i plus maybe large a_i. Targets: bottom A of score_i plus small a_i. We need move from high contribution to low contribution. The resulting d_k = D_target - D_source. We can test pairs and accept if improves.

Optimal delta for pair: We need h(δ)=max c + δ d. Because c,d arrays length L. For a pair, d has special structure but can compute Δ = δ*(d) if step small. We can do discrete optimization:
1. Evaluate h at δ=0 (M), at δ=a_source (or fraction) to check if moving all source to target reduces. We need best δ. Since h convex, use ternary/golden section? But h piecewise linear, not strictly; golden okay. Evaluate h(δ) by max(c + δ d). This is O(L) per eval. We can do ~20 evals per pair. Number of pair tests per outer iteration maybe small (e.g., 8 pairs). L=16383, 8*20*16k=2.6M ops, okay. But Python loops over L are expensive if repeated; use numpy vectorized max(c + delta*d) each eval (C-level). 20*8=160 max ops of 16k ->2.6M in C, fine. 2400s can do many millions.

But we need update c after accepted δ: c += δ*d. d vector computed once O(L), add in numpy. Good.

Candidate pair selection: For outer loop, compute g (gradient w.r.t current max active) and pick source high g, target low g. To optimize δ, maybe use d. We can test a small set:
- top source candidates: indices of top g (maybe 10) and top of d? Also top margin.

```
def optimize_sequence(a, max_time, rng, n_fixed=None):
    a=normalize(a)
    c=conv(a); M=c.max(); best... 
    while time < deadline:
        M = c.max()
        if 2nM < best: update
        # select active weights: top r where r maybe 32 + increasing if stuck
        top = argsort(c)[-r:]; weights = maybe softmax (c_top - floor)/scale or uniform over top
        G = 2*cross_corr(W,a)
        # candidate sources: top G (or where G - min(G)), targets min G
        srcs = top 12 G (but ensure a_s >0)
        tgts = bottom 12 G
        # test pairs, choose best exact delta via grid/ternary
        # apply best if improvement
        # if no improvement or stuck: exponent update
        # occasional recompute c, random perturbation
```

```
def next_power_of_two(m): return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()
def convolve_self(a):
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1; f=next_power_of_two(L)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, f) ? A*A inverse. Use `np.fft.rfft` for speed:
      A= np.fft.rfft(a, f); c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, f)[:L]; return c
```

Let's outline code:

```
import numpy as np, time, math, scipy.fft? maybe use numpy.fft only

def _next_pow2(m):
    if m<=1: return 1
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()

def _conv_self(a):
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1; f=_next_pow2(L)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, f)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, f, n=f)  # maybe n=f
    c=c[:L]
    np.maximum(c,0,out=c)
    return c
```
Check `irfft(A*A, f)` length f. Good.

```
def _normalize(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float)
   a=np.maximum(a,0)
   s=a.sum()
   if not finite or s<1e-12: return zeros? 
   a*=1.0/s
   return a
```

```
def _make_power(n,beta,gamma):
   i=np.arange(n)
   x=n-1-i+gamma
   a=x**(-beta)
   return _normalize(a)

def _make_noisy(base, eps, rng):
   return _normalize((1-eps)*base + eps*rng.random(len(base)))
```

Optimization function:
```
def _optimize(a, max_time, rng, restart=True):
    a=_normalize(a)
    c=_conv_self(a)
    obj=2*n*c.max()
    best_a=a.copy(); best_obj=obj
    no_improve=0
    it=0
    start=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() - start < max_time:
       M=c.max()
       it+=1
       if 2*n*M < best_obj - 1e-12: update
       # coordinate phase
       improved=False
       # active r maybe dynamic: r = min(L, max(32, int(0.004*L)))? For L=16k, 65.
       r = 64 if n<=8192 else 128
       G = _active_gradient(a,c,r)
       # candidate pairs
       pair_best, delta, newM = _coordinate_step(a,c,G,n)
       if pair_best and newM < M - 1e-12:
          # apply
          a = pair_best? _coordinate_step should return new a? We can implement inside to avoid copying? Maybe copy a, update.
          M=newM; improved=True; no_improve=0
       else:
          no_improve+=1
          # periodic exponent
          if no_improve % 20 == 0:
             a2,c2,M2 = _exponent_step(a,G,rng) ...
             if M2<M: a=a2;c=c2;M=M2;improved=True
          if no_improve > 200 or (no_improve>50 and rng.random()<0.01):
             a2,c2,M2 = _anneal_step(a, scale, rng)
             if M2<M or (no_improve>500 and rng.random()<0.2): accept current worsened? keep best separately.
             no_improve=0
       if not improved: no_improve++
       # recompute c every 200 accepted moves
```
Need `_coordinate_step` returns new a,c,M maybe. It should not modify original before success. It can compute d for pairs, find best delta, then apply to a copy. Since a small. To avoid copying many, do after best found.
Pseudo:
```
def _coordinate_step(a,c,G):
  n=len(a); L=len(c); M=c.max()
  # build src candidates
  src_top = np.argsort(G)[-S:]
  src_low? maybe if a_s large and G not top
  src_set = unique top 12 by G + top 6 by a*G
  tgt_set = np.argsort(G)[:T] + bottom 6 by a? maybe
  # score pairs
  pairs=[]
  for s in src_set:
    for t in tgt_set:
      if s==t: continue
      score=max(0,G[s]-G[t]) * a[s]
      pairs.append((score,s,t))
  sort desc, take 12
  best_M=M; best_delta=0; best_s=best_t=None; best_d=None
  frac_list = [0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.7,1.0]
  for score,s,t in pairs:
    ai=a[s]
    if ai<=1e-15: continue
    d=np.zeros(L)
    d[t:t+n] += 2.0*a
    d[s:s+n] -= 2.0*a
    idx=s+t
    # maybe if G difference negative skip? but score.
    for frac in fracs:
       delta=frac*ai
       # linear exact? use temp
       tmp = c + delta*d
       tmp[idx] -= 2.0*delta*delta
       val=tmp.max()
       if val < best_M:
          best_M=val; best_delta=delta; best_s=s; best_t=t
  if best_s is None: return None
  a2=a.copy()
  a2[best_s]-=best_delta; a2[best_t]+=best_delta
  # update c exact
  c2=c.copy()? Could copy c and apply. But we may call then use. To save, return a2 and let caller recompute c? Better update c2 for speed:
  d=np.zeros(L) ...; c2=c + best_delta*d; c2[idx]-=2Δ²; c2=np.maximum(c2,0)
  return a2,c2,best_M
```
Allocating `tmp` for each frac is heavy because it creates new array `c + delta*d`. We can allocate `tmp=np.empty(L)` once per pair and fill:
```
tmp = np.empty(L)
for frac:
  np.multiply(d, delta, out=tmp)
  tmp += c
```

Let's refine active_gradient weights. If we use softmax over top r, weights dominated by highest lags. If plateau with many equal, uniform. If one lag slightly above, it gets more weight. Good. Temperature `temp = max(1e-12, 0.01*cmax)` maybe. But c values ~1e-5, cmax~1e-5, temp~1e-7. Good.
```
def _active_gradient(a,c,r=64,temp_frac=0.01):
   L=len(c); n=len(a); M=c.max()
   if r>=L: idx=np.arange(L); vals=c.copy()
   else: idx=np.argpartition(c, -r)[-r:]
   vals=c[idx]
   if M>0:
      temp=temp_frac*M
      w=np.exp((vals-M)/temp)
   else: w=np.ones_like(vals)
   w/=w.sum()
   G=np.zeros(n)
   for k,wk in zip(idx,w):
      if wk==0: continue
      lo=max(0,int(k)-n+1); hi=min(int(k),n-1)
      if hi<lo: continue
      G[lo:hi+1] += wk * a[int(k)-hi:int(k)-lo+1][::-1]
   return 2*G
```
`argpartition` if r maybe L; if r==L avoid -r error. If L=1? n maybe >1.
When r small, idx unsorted; okay.

Our derivative linear for c_{2t} is 2a_tδ, but actual change +2a_tδ+δ². For source squared c_{2s}: actual -2a_sδ+δ². We omitted +δ² for diagonal changes. Also if s=t no. General exact changes:
- For target t increase: for all k, terms linear 2δ a_{k-t}. Additionally, for k=2t, quadratic +δ² (since a_t^2).
- For source s decrease: linear -2δ a_{k-s}. Additionally, for k=2s, quadratic +δ² (since (a_s-δ)^2 = a_s^2 -2a_sδ+δ²).

```
def _resize(arr, n):
  old=len(arr); if old==n return arr
  x_old=np.linspace(0,1,old)
  x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n)
  return _normalize(np.interp(x_new,x_old,arr))
```

Pseudo code for `_optimize`:
```
def _optimize(a, max_time, rng):
    a=_normalize(a)
    c=_conv_self(a)
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    obj=2.0*n*c.max()
    best_a=a.copy(); best_obj=obj
    start=time.monotonic()
    noimprove=0
    it=0
    accepted=0
    last_recomp=0
    r=64
    # allocate? no
    while time.monotonic() - start < max_time:
        it +=1
        M=c.max()
        if obj < best_obj - 1e-14: ...
        # adapt r
        if noimprove >100: r=min(512, r*2)
        G=_active_gradient(a,c,r)
        found=False
        if it < 500000? no
        step_res=_coordinate_step(a,c,G,rng,n)
        if step_res is not None:
            a,c,M=step_res
            noimprove=0; accepted+=1
            found=True
            if accepted % 20 ==0:
                c=_conv_self(a); M=c.max()
        else:
            noimprove +=1
            # try exponent every 20 noimprove or every 100 it
            if noimprove % 20 ==0:
                res=_exponent_step(a,c,G,rng)
                if res is not None:
                   a,c,M=res; noimprove=0; found=True
                else:
                   # anneal
                   scale = 0.02 * (1.0 + min(1,noimprove/500.0))? 
                   res=_anneal_step(a,rng,scale)
                   if res is not None:
                       a,c,M=res
                       noimprove=0
                       found=True
            if noimprove > 500 and it % 10 ==0:
                # stronger anneal
                scale=0.1
                res=_anneal_step(a,rng,scale)
                if res is not None:
                    a,c,M=res; noimprove=0; found=True
        if time.monotonic() - start > max_time: break
    # final recompute best
    c=_conv_self(best_a); obj=2*n*c.max()
    if obj < best_obj: best_obj=obj
    return best_a, best_obj
```

`_exponent_step`:
```
def _exponent_step(a,c,G,rng):
   n=len(a); M=c.max()
   # baseline current obj M
   best=None
   if G.max()<=0: return None
   Gscale=G.max()
   for eta in (0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4):
       ratio=np.exp(-eta*G/Gscale)
       a2=a*ratio
       a2=np.maximum(a2, 1e-15)
       s=a2.sum(); if s<=0: continue
       a2/=s
       c2=_conv_self(a2); M2=c2.max()
       if M2 < M - 1e-13:
           return a2,c2,M2
   return None
```
This only accepts improvement. If no, none. Could try multiple random? no.

`_anneal_step`:
```
def _anneal_step(a,rng,scale):
   n=len(a)
   # multiplicative noise
   noise=rng.random(n)
   a2=a*(1.0 + scale*(noise-0.5))
   a2=np.maximum(a2,1e-15)
   a2/=a2.sum()
   c2=_conv_self(a2); M2=c2.max()
   return a2,c2,M2
```
We accept regardless (to escape). But if it's much worse, coordinate may take long to recover; since best saved. Could scale large and worsen too much; use scale 0.05. Maybe only accept if not worse than factor 1.05? But to escape local, can accept 5% worse. Use:
```
if M2 < best Current? We don't have best in function. Could pass current best obj and allow up to 1.05*best? Simpler accept always when triggered; if bad, subsequent coordinate/exponent from bad maybe waste time. Instead accept only if M2 < current M * (1 + 0.1) or noimprove high. We can return only if M2 < M + 0.05*best_obj? In _anneal_step pass `threshold` and accept if M2<threshold. But sometimes need accept worsened. Implement `_anneal_step(a,rng,scale,max_M)` returns if M2<=max_M.
```

```
W = np.zeros(L); W[idx]=weights
Ar = a[::-1]
fsize = _next_pow2(L+n-1)
Wf = np.fft.rfft(W, fsize)
Af = np.fft.rfft(Ar, fsize)
conv = np.fft.irfft(Wf*Af, fsize)
G = 2 * conv[n-1 : 2*n-1]? C_s s=0..n-1 -> indices n-1+s length n.
```
This is O(fsize log fsize) with C-level, much faster and no Python loops. Great. We'll use this. Need ensure weights sum 1; if not, G scale. We can normalize weights sum1. Convolution values small. We need `idx` from top r. Build W array length L each gradient; O(L). Fine.

Let's implement `_active_gradient_fft(a,c,r=128,temp_frac=0.01)`:
```
n=len(a); L=2*n-1

else: idx=np.argpartition(c, -r)[-r:]
vals=c[idx]
M=vals.max()
temp=temp_frac*max(M,1e-300)
w=np.exp((vals-M)/temp); w/=w.sum()
W=np.zeros(L); W[idx]=w
Ar=a[::-1]
fsize=_next_pow2(L+n-1)
WF=np.fft.rfft(W,fsize)
AF=np.fft.rfft(Ar,fsize)
conv=np.fft.irfft(WF*AF,fsize)
G=2.0*conv[n-1:n-1+n]
np.maximum(G,0,out=G) # numerical
return G
```

```
def _coordinate_step(a,c,G,rng,fracs):
  n=len(a); L=len(c); M=c.max()
  argsG=np.argsort(G)
  top20=argsG[-20:]; bot20=argsG[:20]
  topA=np.argsort(a)[-10:]; botA=np.argsort(a)[:10]
  # random from top100/bot100
  br_top=argsG[-100:]; br_bot=argsG[:100]
  src_candidates = list top20 + topA
  tgt_candidates = list bot20 + botA
  add 5 random from each
  src_idx=np.unique(np.asarray(src_candidates))
  tgt_idx=np.unique(np.asarray(tgt_candidates))
  # sort src by G descending, tgt ascending to prioritize? We'll score pairs anyway.
  pairs=[]
  sqrt_eps=1e-14
  for s in src_idx:
    ai=a[s]
    if ai <= 0: continue
    s_score_base=np.sqrt(ai+sqrt_eps)
    gs=G[s]
    for t in tgt_idx:
       if s==t: continue
       diff=gs-G[t]
       if diff <= 1e-18: continue
       score=diff*s_score_base
       pairs.append((score,int(s),int(t)))
  pairs.sort(reverse=True)
  pairs=pairs[:12]
  two_a=2.0*a
  diff=np.zeros(L); tmp=np.empty(L)
  best_M=M; best_s=-1; best_t=-1; best_delta=0.0
  for score,s,t in pairs:
    cross=s+t; sq_s=2*s; sq_t=2*t
    diff.fill(0.0)
    diff[t:t+n] += two_a
    diff[s:s+n] -= two_a
    ai=a[s]
    # Maybe compute quick if full? We'll loop fractions.
    for frac in fracs:
      delta=frac*ai
      if delta <= 1e-18: continue
      np.multiply(diff, delta, out=tmp)
      tmp += c
      # apply quadratics
      tmp[cross] -= 2.0*delta*delta
      tmp[sq_t] += delta*delta
      tmp[sq_s] += delta*delta
      # if indices coincide? They don't but if do, operations cumulative correct.
      val=float(tmp.max())
      if val < best_M:
          best_M=val; best_s=s; best_t=t; best_delta=delta
  if best_s<0: return None
  a2=a.copy()
  a2[best_s]-=best_delta; a2[best_t]+=best_delta
  # clamp tiny negative
  if a2[best_s] < 0: a2[best_s]=0.0
  # recompute c2 from old a,c,best to avoid drift? Use diff again
  c2=c.copy()
  diff.fill(0.0); diff[best_t:best_t+n]+=two_a; diff[best_s:best_s+n]-=two_a
  delta=best_delta
  np.multiply(diff, delta, out=tmp)
  tmp += c2
  tmp[best_s+best_t] -= 2*delta*delta
  tmp[2*best_t] += delta*delta
  tmp[2*best_s] += delta*delta
  c2 = np.maximum(tmp,0.0)  # this copies? np.maximum returns new if out not set
  # Actually tmp after += c2; use np.maximum(tmp,0,out=tmp); c2=tmp
  return a2,c2,best_M
```

Pseudo code exact:
```
def _optimize(a0, max_time, rng):
    a=_normalize(a0.copy())
    c=_conv_self(a); n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    M=c.max(); obj=2*n*M
    best_a=a.copy(); best_obj=obj
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+max_time
    it=0; no=0; rec=0
    fracs=(0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.7,1.0)
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it+=1
        M0=c.max()
        obj_cur=2*n*M0
        if obj_cur < best_obj - 1e-12:
            best_obj=obj_cur
            best_a=a.copy()
        # if current far above best, reset
        if obj_cur > best_obj*1.05 and it>10:
            a=best_a.copy(); c=_conv_self(a); M=c.max(); no=0; continue
        r=128
        if no>100: r=256
        if no>300: r=512
        G=_active_gradient(a,c,r)
        res=_coordinate_step(a,c,G,rng,fracs)
        if res is not None:
            a,c,M=res
            no=0
            rec+=1
            if rec%25==0:
                c=_conv_self(a); M=c.max()
            continue
        no +=1
        if no % 20 ==0:
            res=_exponent_step(a,c,G,rng)
            if res is not None:
                a,c,M=res; no=0; rec+=1; continue
            # anneal
            scale=0.03
            max_allowed=max(2*n*M, best_obj)*1.05
            res=_anneal_step(a,rng,scale)
            if res is not None and 2*n*M_new? need res M
               a,c,M=res; no=0; continue
        if no > 300 and it % 20 ==0:
            # reset to best
            a=best_a.copy(); c=_conv_self(a); M=c.max(); no=1
    c=_conv_self(best_a); obj=2*n*c.max(); if obj<best_obj: best_obj=obj
    return best_a,best_obj
```

Double-check `_resize`:
```
def _resize(arr, n):
   old=len(arr)
   if old==0: return np.zeros(n)
   if old==n: return arr.copy()
   xold=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,old)
   xnew=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
   y=np.interp(xnew,xold,arr)
   return _normalize(y)
```

Now, small issue: In `_active_gradient_fft`, weights sum to one. If we use top 512 lags with softmax, G scale ~M*weight_per. Exponent eta divides by Gscale=max G, so update bounded. Good.

Let's final.

Wait: The problem statement says "Your function will have 2400 seconds to run, and after that it has to have returned the best sequence it found. If after 2400 seconds it has not returned anything, it will be terminated with negative infinity points." It may not pass budget_s? But our default 2400. Good.

If `budget_s` passed smaller, our end uses it. Good.

Now, one thought: The evaluation function clamps values to min(1000). Our sum1 values <1. Good. If final best_a normalized sum1 but some values maybe exactly 0; okay.

Let's ensure target upper bound: If our best objective after final polish is 1.503, we pass. We don't know. To further safety, we can explicitly aim by running multiple basins. The code does.

Maybe we should include a final simple random perturbation of best and recompute if it improves, using remaining time in chunks. `_optimize` already anneal.

Let's inspect `_optimize` reset condition: if `obj_cur > best_obj*1.05 and it>10`, reset. If best_obj internal from candidate is 1.506, current after anneal maybe 1.6, reset. Good. If coordinate improvements only on current below best, update best. If current starts at best, anneal may worsen and coordinate may not recover within threshold because threshold max_M*1.05; if accepted 1.581, current obj >best*1.05? best1.506, 1.05=1.581. It may reset soon if no improvement; could waste. But noimprove low after anneal; coordinate may find improvement. If not, reset after 500. Fine.

Now, code no closures. Let's produce final.
</think>

```python
"""
Search for non-negative sequences minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / sum(a)^2.

The normalizing sum is taken to be 1, so the objective is 2*n*max(conv(a,a)).
The search uses:
- several inexpensive analytic initializers (power-law boundary profiles,
  noisy hybrids, and the previous best sequence if available),
- exact coordinate transfers with full piecewise-quadratic objective checks,
- FFT-based subgradient information over the dominant convolution coefficients,
- exponential-direction steps and controlled annealing to escape local basins.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

COORD_FRACS = (0.005, 0.01, 0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 1.00)


def _next_pow2(m):
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def _normalize(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if a.size == 0:
        return array_dummy
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.zeros_like(a, dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s


# Used only if a bizarre input produces an empty array after sanitization.
array_dummy = np.zeros(1, dtype=np.float64)


def _resize(a, n):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    old = a.size
    if old == 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if old == n:
        return a.copy()
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, old)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    y = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    return _normalize(y)


def _conv_self(a):
    a = np.ascontiguousarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    fsize = _next_pow2(L)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, fsize)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, fsize)
    c = c[:L]
    np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
    return c


def _objective_normalized(a):
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    c = _conv_self(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    mx = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * mx)


def _make_power(n, beta, gamma, orientation=1):
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if orientation > 0:
        x = n - 1.0 - i + gamma
    else:
        x = i + gamma
    a = np.power(np.maximum(x, 1e-12), -beta)
    return _normalize(a)


def _active_gradient(a, c, r=128, temp_frac=0.01):
    """
    Gradient of the smoothly weighted sum of the top-r convolution coefficients.
    Returns G[s] = 2 * sum_k W[k] a[k-s], with W over the selected peaks.
    """
    n = a.size
    L = c.size
    r = int(max(1, min(r, L)))
    if r >= L:
        idx = np.arange(L)
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -r)[-r:]
    vals = c[idx]
    mx = float(vals.max())
    temp = max(1e-300, temp_frac * mx)
    w = np.exp((vals - mx) / temp)
    ws = float(w.sum())
    if ws <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(ws):
        w = np.ones_like(vals)
        ws = float(w.sum())
    w = w / ws

    W = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    W[idx] = w

    # Convolution with reversed a gives:
    # H[m] = sum_k W[k] a[n-1-(m-k)]
    # and we need G[s]/2 = H[n-1+s].
    Ar = a[::-1]
    fsize = _next_pow2(L + n - 1)
    WF = np.fft.rfft(W, fsize)
    AF = np.fft.rfft(Ar, fsize)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(WF * AF, fsize)
    G = 2.0 * conv[n - 1:n - 1 + n]
    np.maximum(G, 0.0, out=G)
    return G


def _coordinate_step(a, c, G, rng, test_pairs=12):
    """
    Try a few exact two-coordinate transfers.
    A transfer moves mass from source s to target t:
        a[s] -= delta, a[t] += delta.
    The update of conv(a,a) is exact for that transfer, including quadratic
    correction terms at lags 2*s, 2*t, and s+t.
    Returns (a_new, c_new, M_new) if an improving move is found, else None.
    """
    n = a.size
    L = c.size
    if n < 2:
        return None
    M = float(c.max())
    tol = 1e-13

    argsG = np.argsort(G)
    top_count = 16
    bot_count = 16
    a_count = 8
    rnd_count = 6

    topG = argsG[-top_count:]
    botG = argsG[:bot_count]
    argsA = np.argsort(a)
    topA = argsA[-a_count:]
    botA = argsA[:a_count]

    br_top = argsG[-100:]
    br_bot = argsG[:100]
    rnd_top = br_top[rng.integers(0, br_top.size, size=rnd_count)]
    rnd_bot = br_bot[rng.integers(0, br_bot.size, size=rnd_count)]

    src_arr = np.concatenate((topG, topA, rnd_top))
    tgt_arr = np.concatenate((botG, botA, rnd_bot))
    src_arr = np.unique(src_arr)
    tgt_arr = np.unique(tgt_arr)

    pairs = []
    eps = 1e-14
    for s in src_arr:
        s = int(s)
        ai = float(a[s])
        if ai <= 0.0:
            continue
        gs = float(G[s])
        sq_ai = float(np.sqrt(ai + eps))
        for t in tgt_arr:
            t = int(t)
            if s == t:
                continue
            dg = gs - float(G[t])
            if dg <= 0.0:
                continue
            score = dg * sq_ai
            pairs.append((score, s, t))

    if not pairs:
        return None

    pairs.sort(reverse=True)
    pairs = pairs[:max(1, int(test_pairs))]

    two_a = 2.0 * a
    diff = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    tmp = np.empty(L, dtype=np.float64)

    best_M = M
    best_s = -1
    best_t = -1
    best_delta = 0.0

    for _, s, t in pairs:
        s = int(s)
        t = int(t)
        ai = float(a[s])
        if ai <= 0.0:
            continue

        cross = s + t
        sq_s = 2 * s
        sq_t = 2 * t

        diff.fill(0.0)
        diff[t:t + n] += two_a
        diff[s:s + n] -= two_a

        for frac in COORD_FRACS:
            delta = float(frac) * ai
            if delta <= 0.0:
                continue
            np.multiply(diff, delta, out=tmp)
            tmp += c
            tmp[cross] -= 2.0 * delta * delta
            tmp[sq_t] += delta * delta
            tmp[sq_s] += delta * delta
            val = float(tmp.max())
            if val < best_M - tol:
                best_M = val
                best_s = s
                best_t = t
                best_delta = delta

    if best_s < 0:
        return None

    a2 = a.copy()
    a2[best_s] = max(0.0, a[best_s] - best_delta)
    a2[best_t] = a[best_t] + best_delta

    c2 = np.empty(L, dtype=np.float64)
    diff.fill(0.0)
    diff[best_t:best_t + n] += two_a
    diff[best_s:best_s + n] -= two_a

    delta = best_delta
    np.multiply(diff, delta, out=c2)
    c2 += c
    cross = best_s + best_t
    c2[cross] -= 2.0 * delta * delta
    c2[2 * best_t] += delta * delta
    c2[2 * best_s] += delta * delta
    np.maximum(c2, 0.0, out=c2)

    return a2, c2, best_M


def _exponent_step(a, c, G):
    """
    Multiplicative reweighting in the -gradient direction for the weighted
    peak-convolution surrogate. Accept only if exact objective improves.
    """
    n = a.size
    M = float(c.max())
    gmax = float(G.max())
    if gmax <= 1e-300:
        return None

    for eta in (0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.40):
        ratio = np.exp(-eta * G / gmax)
        a2 = a * ratio
        a2 = np.maximum(a2, 1e-18)
        s = float(a2.sum())
        if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
            continue
        a2 /= s
        c2 = _conv_self(a2)
        M2 = float(c2.max())
        if M2 < M - 1e-13:
            return a2, c2, M2
    return None


def _anneal_step(a, rng, scale, max_M):
    """
    Multiplicative multiplicative random perturbation, accepted only if not
    much worse than max_M.
    """
    n = a.size
    noise = rng.random(n)
    factor = 1.0 + float(scale) * (noise - 0.5)
    a2 = np.maximum(a * factor, 1e-18)
    s = float(a2.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return None
    a2 /= s
    c2 = _conv_self(a2)
    M2 = float(c2.max())
    if np.isfinite(M2) and M2 <= max_M:
        return a2, c2, M2
    return None


def _optimize(a0, max_time, rng):
    """
    Local stochastic optimizer: exact coordinate line search, active FFT
    subgradients, exponential moves, and limited annealing.
    """
    a = _normalize(a0.copy())
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a0, np.inf
    c = _conv_self(a)
    M = float(c.max())
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_obj = 2.0 * n * M
    if not np.isfinite(best_obj):
        best_a = np.ones(n) / n
        best_obj = 2.0

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(max_time))

    it = 0
    no_improve = 0
    accepted = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1
        M = float(c.max())
        obj_cur = 2.0 * n * M
        if np.isfinite(obj_cur) and obj_cur < best_obj - 1e-12:
            best_obj = obj_cur
            best_a = a.copy()

        # If the working point drifts far from the best one, return to it.
        if it > 10 and obj_cur > 1.05 * best_obj:
            a = best_a.copy()
            c = _conv_self(a)
            M = float(c.max())
            no_improve = 0
            continue

        r = 128
        if no_improve > 100:
            r = 256
        if no_improve > 300:
            r = 512

        G = _active_gradient(a, c, r=r)

        test_pairs = 12
        if no_improve > 100:
            test_pairs = 6

        res = _coordinate_step(a, c, G, rng, test_pairs=test_pairs)
        if res is not None:
            a, c, M = res
            no_improve = 0
            accepted += 1
            if accepted % 25 == 0:
                c = _conv_self(a)
                M = float(c.max())
            continue

        no_improve += 1

        if no_improve % 20 == 0:
            res = _exponent_step(a, c, G)
            if res is not None:
                a, c, M = res
                no_improve = 0
                accepted += 1
                continue

            best_M = best_obj / (2.0 * n)
            scale = 0.03 + 0.05 * min(1.0, no_improve / 300.0)
            slack = 1.05 + 0.20 * min(1.0, no_improve / 500.0)
            max_allowed = max(M, best_M) * slack
            res = _anneal_step(a, rng, scale, max_allowed)
            if res is not None:
                a, c, M = res
                no_improve = 0
                continue

        if no_improve > 400 and it % 10 == 0:
            # Periodically return to the best point to avoid wandering.
            a = best_a.copy()
            c = _conv_self(a)
            M = float(c.max())
            no_improve = 1

    c = _conv_self(best_a)
    obj_final = 2.0 * n * float(c.max())
    if np.isfinite(obj_final) and obj_final < best_obj:
        best_obj = obj_final
    return best_a, best_obj


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    try:
        total_budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        total_budget = 2400.0
    end_time = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, total_budget - 60.0)

    n = 8192
    candidates = []

    # Use the previous construction if present.
    parent = None
    try:
        seq = height_sequence_1
        arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if arr.size > 0 and float(arr.sum()) > 1e-12:
            if arr.size != n:
                arr = _resize(arr, n)
            parent = _normalize(arr)
            if parent.size == n:
                candidates.append(parent)
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    # Low-cost analytic starts.  The one-sided critical power-law profile is
    # already close in the right mathematical scaling.
    for beta in (0.45, 0.48, 0.50, 0.52, 0.55):
        for gamma in (0.25, 0.50, 1.00):
            candidates.append(_make_power(n, beta, gamma, orientation=1))
            candidates.append(_make_power(n, beta, gamma, orientation=-1))

    infos = []
    counter = 0
    for base in candidates:
        if base.size != n:
            continue
        a = _normalize(base)
        c = _conv_self(a)
        obj = _objective_normalized(a)
        if np.isfinite(obj):
            infos.append((float(obj), counter, a, c))
            counter += 1

    if not infos:
        a = np.ones(n) / n
        return [float(x) for x in a]

    infos.sort()

    # Add noisy hybrids of the best few starts.
    top_for_noise = [x[2] for x in infos[:3]]
    for base in top_for_noise:
        for eps in (0.01, 0.02, 0.05):
            a2 = _normalize((1.0 - eps) * base + eps * rng.random(n))
            obj2 = _objective_normalized(a2)
            if np.isfinite(obj2):
                infos.append((float(obj2), counter, a2, _conv_self(a2)))
                counter += 1
    infos.sort()

    global_best_a = None
    global_best_obj = np.inf

    # Keep the initial best as a safe fallback.
    global_best_obj = float(infos[0][0])
    global_best_a = np.array(infos[0][2], copy=True)

    # Optimize the most promising distinct starts.  Time is divided among them
    # to avoid committing all budget to one basin.
    fractions = (0.45, 0.30, 0.25)
    remaining = max(1.0, end_time - time.monotonic())

    for i, frac in enumerate(fractions):
        if i >= len(infos):
            break
        if time.monotonic() >= end_time:
            break
        t = min(frac * remaining, max(5.0, end_time - time.monotonic()))
        if t < 5.0:
            break
        obj0, _, a0, _ = infos[i]
        a_opt, obj_opt = _optimize(a0, t, rng)
        if np.isfinite(obj_opt) and obj_opt < global_best_obj:
            global_best_obj = obj_opt
            global_best_a = np.array(a_opt, copy=True)
        remaining = max(0.0, end_time - time.monotonic())

    # Final polishing from the globally best sequence found so far.
    if time.monotonic() < end_time and global_best_a is not None:
        t = max(0.0, end_time - time.monotonic())
        if t > 2.0:
            a_opt, obj_opt = _optimize(global_best_a, t, rng)
            if np.isfinite(obj_opt) and obj_opt < global_best_obj:
                global_best_obj = obj_opt
                global_best_a = np.array(a_opt, copy=True)

    if global_best_a is None:
        global_best_a = np.ones(n) / n

    global_best_a = _normalize(global_best_a)
    final_list = [float(x) for x in global_best_a]

    # Final consistency check.  The evaluator will recompute the objective,
    # but we print our own FFT estimate and, if feasible, the official value.
    try:
        c_final = _conv_self(global_best_a)
        internal_obj = 2.0 * n * float(c_final.max())
        print("Internal final objective: %.9f, n=%d" % (internal_obj, n))
    except Exception:
        internal_obj = np.inf

    try:
        official = evaluate_sequence(final_list)
        if np.isfinite(official):
            print("Official verified objective: %.9f, n=%d" % (official, n))
        else:
            print("Official verification returned non-finite; using last internal result.")
    except NameError:
        pass
    except Exception:
        pass

    return final_list
```
